Here we write down the one-loop integral measure applicable to the diagram such as the

{\cal A}_{01}^{\prime(1)}
depicted in Fig.
9
(left).
Appealing to the Lorentz invariance of the loop measure, we write this in the
*
-frame as

d\Pi_{2}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}dc_{3}^{*}d\phi_{3}^{*}\frac{{q_{3}^{*}}_{r}}{4(E_{n}^{*}-E_{U}^{*})}\ ,

(89)

where

{q_{3}^{*}}_{r}=(M_{B}/2)\{[1-(M_{3}^{2}+M_{4}^{2})/M_{B}^{2}]^{2}-4M_{3}^{2}M_{4}^{2}/M_{B}^{4}\}^{1/2}

with
M_{3,4}=q_{3,4}^{2}
.

We point out a subtlety in the maximum value of
{q}_{U_{r}}
in Fig.
9
.
Since the left-cut also has to be enforced, 4-momentum conservation at the left-most vertex puts an additional constraint,
namely, we must now also have
{q}_{U_{r}}\leq{{\tilde{q}}_{U_{r}}}^{(max)}

where
{{\tilde{q}}_{U_{r}}}^{(max)}=(M_{n}/2)\{[1+(M_{U}^{2}-(M_{m}+M_{U})^{2})/M_{n}^{2}]^{2}-4M_{U}^{2}/M_{n}^{2}\}^{1/2}
,
in addition to
{q}_{U_{r}}\leq{q}_{U_{r}}^{(max)}
as earlier for the right-cut.
We must thus take the stricter upper-bound of these, i.e. we have the

{q}_{U_{r}}
domain of integration for
{\cal A}_{01}^{\prime(1)}
now given as

{q}_{U_{r}}\in\left(0,{\rm min}\{{q}_{U_{r}}^{(max)},{{\tilde{q}}_{U_{r}}}^{(max)}\}\right)
, while the other integrations are over the usual full range.

F.3
The 2-body phase-space variables and measure (for
{\cal X}
scattering)

Our choice of the final-state phase-space variables has similarities with the considerations of Sec.
F.1
,
but now for scattering it is a 2-body phase-space, unlike being 3-body phase-space earlier.
One change for scattering is that instead of the final state momentum
q_{U}
we now have the initial state momentum
k_{i}
.
Another change is that in decay, we could analyze the process in the
{\cal X}
rest-frame without any loss of generality,
but in scattering, we have to generalize this to have arbitrary
{p_{n}}_{r}
, i.e. we no longer take the lab-frame to be the
{\cal X}
rest-frame here.
We make these generalizations in our implementation of
d\Pi_{2}
for our scattering processes SC-1 and SC-2 discussed in Sec.
4
.

Similar to the procedure of Sec.
F.2
, we now take the incoming momenta
{\bf p}_{n}
in terms of the independent
({p_{n}}_{r},c_{n},\phi_{n})
,
and
{\bf k}_{i}
in terms of the independent variables
({k_{i}}_{r},c_{i},\phi_{i})
.
For unpolarized
{\cal X}
scattering the matrix element will have no
\phi_{n}
dependence and we can integrate it trivially to get a factor of
2\pi
,
and conveniently set
\phi_{n}=0
.

The Lorentz invariance of
d\Pi_{2}
allows us to compute it in any frame, and we choose to compute it in the
center-of-momentum (CM) frame, which is the rest-frame of
k^{\prime}_{A}=p_{n}+k_{i}
.
9
9

9

This has parallels to the *-frame in Sec.
F.1
, which was the rest-frame of
k_{B}
.

We now write

d\Pi_{2}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}dc_{1}^{CM}d\phi^{CM}_{1}\frac{{q_{1}}_{r}^{CM}}{4M_{A}}\,\left[\frac{1}{2}\right]_{SC-2}\ ,

(90)

where
\theta_{1}\equiv\theta_{q_{1}}
,
\phi_{1}\equiv\phi_{q_{1}}
with
c_{1}\equiv\cos{\theta_{1}}
,
M_{A}^{2}={k^{\prime}_{A}}^{2}
.
We have
M_{A}=E_{CM}=E^{CM}_{n}+E_{i}^{CM}
,
and

{q_{1}}_{r}^{CM}=(M_{A}/2)\,\{[1+(M_{2}^{2}-M_{1}^{2})/M_{A}^{2}]^{2}-4M_{2}^{2}/M_{A}^{2}\}^{1/2}
,
and since
M_{A}
is frame-independent, we also have

M_{A}^{2}=M_{n}^{2}+M_{i}^{2}+2(E_{n}E_{i}-{p_{n}}_{r}{k_{i}}_{r}c_{ni})
, and without loss of generality we choose
{\bf p}_{n}
to be along the z-axis, and therefore,
c_{ni}=c_{i}
.
We include an extra factor of
1/2
only for SC-2 due to two identical
{D}
in the final state.

F.4
The loop variables and measure (for
{\cal X}
scattering)

Our implementation of the loop integration measure in scattering
closely parallels the decay case in Sec.
F.2
, and we discuss here how we adapt this for scattering.
As mentioned in Sec.
F.3
we generalize to have the scattering
{p_{n}}_{r}\neq 0
here too.

F.4.1
Two-loop measure

We construct the
{\oasterisk}
frame exactly as discussed before in Sec.
F.2
.
But using the Lorentz invariance of the measure, we choose to now take as the independent variable

{{\bf q}^{\prime}_{U}}
variables in the
{\oasterisk}
frame (instead of the lab frame earlier).
As before we take
{\bf q}_{3}
independent variables also in the
{\oasterisk}
frame.
We can then write the loop integration measure for the scattering channels as

d\tilde{\Pi}^{\prime}_{3}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{5}}\frac{1}{2}d{c^{\prime}_{U}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}d{\phi^{\prime}_{U}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}d{{{q^{\prime}}_{\!\!U}}_{\!r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}}dc^{\oasterisk}_{3}d\phi^{\oasterisk}_{3}\frac{{{{q^{\prime}}_{\!\!U}}_{\!r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}}^{2}}{2{E^{\prime}_{U}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}}\frac{{q_{3}}_{r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}}{4({E_{n}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}-{E^{\prime}_{U}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}})}\ ,

(91)

The domain of integration for these variables are the usual full range, except for
{{{q^{\prime}}_{\!\!U}}_{\!r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}}\in(0,{{{q^{\prime}}_{\!\!U}}_{\!r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}}^{(max)})
.
Now,
{{{q^{\prime}}_{\!\!U}}_{\!r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}}^{(max)}
is determined as follows.
We first compute the Lorentz invariant

{M_{B}^{\prime}}^{2}=(p_{n}-q_{U}^{\prime})^{2}=({E_{n}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}-{E^{\prime}_{U}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}})^{2}=M_{n}^{2}+M_{U}^{2}+2({{{q^{\prime}}_{\!\!U}}_{\!r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}}^{2}-{E_{n}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}{E^{\prime}_{U}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}})
.
But we also have from 4-momentum conservation
M_{B}^{\prime}={E_{3}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}+{E_{4}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}
.
We realize that
{{{q^{\prime}}_{\!\!U}}_{\!r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}}
attains its maximum value when
{q_{3}}_{r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}=0
, i.e. when
M_{B}^{\prime}=2M_{D}
,
and we obtain

{{{q^{\prime}}_{\!\!U}}_{\!r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}}^{(max)}=(M_{n}^{2}/(4M_{D}))\{[1+(M_{U}^{2}-4M_{D}^{2})/M_{n}^{2}]^{2}-4M_{U}^{2}/M_{n}^{2}\}^{1/2}
.

Given the above independent momenta in the
{\oasterisk}
frame, we transform all the loop momenta to the lab-frame
using the boost transformation that we have already given in Sec.
F.2
.
With all the momenta now available in the lab-frame, we compute the 4-momentum dot-products,
and the matrix element in terms of those.
We then fold in the loop integration measure discussed here and obtain the required integrals numerically.

F.4.2
One-loop measure

For the multiple operator contribution in SC-2, we have
p_{n}+k_{i}=q_{3}+q_{4}
as can be seen from the crossed Fig.
8
.
Thus, it would be most convenient to pick the independent loop momenta in the CM frame as the
{\bf q}_{3}
and
{\bf q}_{4}
are back-to-back in this frame.
We follow our analysis in Sec.
F.3
and write analogous to Eq. (
90
) the one-loop measure for this case as

d\Pi^{\prime}_{2}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}dc_{3}^{CM}d\phi^{CM}_{3}\frac{{q_{3}}_{r}^{CM}}{4M_{A}}\ ,

(92)

where
q_{3}=q_{m}
is the
{\cal X}_{m}
momentum.

Appendix G
The Decay and Scattering Rates

Here we give details on the tree-loop interference terms
putting together the matrix element and phase-space measure discussed above
in Appendices
E
and
F
respectively,
and about the numerical evaluation of the decay and scattering rate differences.

G.1
The tree-level decay rate

To obtain the total decay width, we fold in the 3-body phase-space element and integrate.
We evaluate the 3-body phase-space element equivalently as a cut of a loop diagram as

d\Pi_{3}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\frac{d^{4}q_{U}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}q_{1}}{(2\pi)^{4}}(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{\rm cut}_{12U}\ ,\quad{\rm where}\ \delta^{\rm cut}_{12U}\equiv\delta(q_{U}^{2}-M_{U}^{2})\delta(q_{1}^{2}-M_{D}^{2})\delta(q_{2}^{2}-M_{D}^{2})\ ,

(93)

and we have a factor of 1/2
due to the two identical
{D}
in the final state.
We
explicitly show in Appendices
E.2.1
and
E.3.2

that
|M^{12}|^{2}=L_{12{}}
, the latter being the corresponding loop expression.
We take
q_{1},q_{U}
as the independent momenta, and
the full details on our choice of independent 3-body phase-space variables and integrations over them are given in
Appendix
F.1
.
As explained there, we have integrated over the
d^{4}q_{2}/(2\pi)^{4}
and enforced the overall energy-momentum conserving

\delta
-function
(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{4}(p_{n}-(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{U}))
.

We have
|M^{12}_{0}|^{2}_{\tau\mu}=(|M^{12}_{0}|^{2}_{\mu\tau})|_{M_{D}\rightarrow(-M_{D})}
.
Because the trace of an odd number of
\gamma
matrices is zero, we have the
M_{D}
dependence like

(|M^{12}_{0}|^{2}_{\tau\mu})\supset-M_{D}^{2}(...)
,
which
implies that
|M^{12}_{0}|^{2}_{\tau\mu}=|M^{12}_{0}|^{2}_{\mu\tau}
and therefore
(|M^{12}_{0}|^{2}_{\mu\tau})^{*}=(|M^{12}_{0}|^{2}_{\mu\tau})
.
We also have
|M^{12}_{0}|^{2}_{\mu\tau}=|M_{0}^{c\,12}|^{2}_{\mu\tau}
(cf. Appendix
E.2.1
).
We can also show
|{{\cal M}_{0}^{c\,\chi U\,\!}}|^{2}_{\mu\tau}=|{{\cal M}_{0}^{\chi U\,\!}}|^{2}_{\tau\mu}={|{{\cal M}_{0}^{\chi U\,\!}}|^{2}_{\mu\tau}}^{\!\!\!\!*}
.
From these relations, we can infer that
|{{\cal A}^{n}_{0}}|^{2}=|{{\cal A}^{c\,n}_{0}}|^{2}
,
which implies
\Gamma_{0}^{n}=\Gamma_{0}^{cn}
,
showing explicitly that the LO decay rate is the same for the process and the conjugate process and that there is no baryon asymmetry at LO.
The total decay width is the sum of the widths for the process and the conjugate process,
and to obtain the leading contribution to the baryon asymmetry,
it is sufficient to take for the total width
\Gamma_{{\cal X}_{n}}\approx\Gamma_{0}^{n}+\Gamma_{0}^{cn}=2\Gamma_{0}^{n}
,
since there is no asymmetry at LO.

For the numerical computation of the integrals,
we scale all dimensionful quantities by
M_{n}

and define dimensionless momenta
\hat{p}_{i}\equiv p_{i}/M_{n}
and dimensionless masses
\hat{M}_{i}\equiv M_{i}/M_{n}
,
strip off the couplings
and define (dimensionless) coupling-independent traces and integrals

\displaystyle(\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}})_{nn}^{\mu\tau}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}[\gamma^{\mu}P_{\lambda}(\hat{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.03125pt{\hfil/\hfil}\hss}p_{n}+\hat{M}_{n})\gamma^{\tau}P_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\hat{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/_{U}+\hat{M}_{U})]\ ,

(94)

\displaystyle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d\hat{\Pi}_{3}\,\frac{1}{2}(\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}})_{nn}^{\mu\tau}\,C_{00}\,(\hat{L}_{12{}})_{\mu\tau}\ ,

(95)

where
P_{\lambda}
is the chirality projection operator with
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}=\{L,R\}
.
Including the couplings we have

|{{\cal\hat{M}}_{0}^{\chi U\,\!}}|^{2}=\sum_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\hat{g}^{*}_{\lambda}\hat{g}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}
with hatted momenta and masses,
and,

|\tilde{g}|^{2}\,\hat{L}_{12{}}=|\hat{M}^{12}|^{2}
is
with hatted momenta and masses.
Including the couplings, we define the dimensionless

\displaystyle f_{00}^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\tilde{g}|^{2}\left(|\hat{g}_{L_{n}}|^{2}{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{LL}+|\hat{g}_{R_{n}}|^{2}{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{RR}+\hat{g}_{L_{n}}^{*}\hat{g}_{R_{n}}{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{LR}+\hat{g}_{R_{n}}^{*}\hat{g}_{L_{n}}{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{RL}\right)\ ,

(96)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\tilde{g}|^{2}\left[(|\hat{g}_{L_{n}}|^{2}+|\hat{g}_{R_{n}}|^{2})\,{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{LL}+(\hat{g}_{L_{n}}^{*}\hat{g}_{R_{n}}+\hat{g}_{R_{n}}^{*}\hat{g}_{L_{n}})\,{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{LR}\right]\ .

In terms of this we write the LO decay rate as in Eq. (
10
).

We find
\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{RR}=(\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{LL})^{*}
,
\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{RL}=\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{LR}=(\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{LR})^{*}
,
which imply
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{RR}=({{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{LL})^{*}
,
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{RL}={{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{LR}=({{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{LR})^{*}
.
We also find that
(\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{LL}-\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{RR})^{\mu\tau}=2i\epsilon^{\mu\tau\nu\sigma}{p_{n}}_{\nu}{q_{U}}_{\sigma}
, and when contracted with the
symmetric
\hat{L}_{12{\mu\tau}}
, the RHS gives zero, which implies that
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{LL}={{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{RR}
,
and since the symmetric piece is real, these integrals are real.
Therefore,
f_{00}^{n}
is real as required.

The single operator decay rate difference

({{\cal M}_{1(i)}^{\chi U\,\!}})

{({{\cal M}_{1(i)}^{\chi U\,\!}})}^{\sigma\mu\nu}_{nm}\equiv\bar{u}(q_{U}){\phantom{\,}\bar{G}_{V}^{m}\phantom{\,\!}}^{\sigma}({\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.03125pt{\hfil/\hfil}\hss}p^{\prime(i)}_{n}}+M_{m}){\phantom{\,}\bar{G}_{\Lambda}^{m}\phantom{\,\!}}^{\mu}(\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/^{\prime}_{U}+M_{U}){G_{\Lambda}^{n}}^{\nu}u(p_{n})\ .

We give the details of the loop integration measure

As an example, we highlight the following aspects of diagram A.
The color factor is

C_{01}^{(A)}=\epsilon^{abc}\epsilon^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c}\epsilon^{abc^{\prime}}\epsilon^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}/(\sqrt{6})^{2}=12/(\sqrt{6})^{2}=2\ ,

using

The

and

We can show using an identical argument as earlier that

(L_{34})_{\sigma\nu}=(L^{c}_{34})_{\sigma\nu}

In a similar way, we compute these factors for the other diagrams also, but do not give the details here.

We obtain
the decay rate difference as

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{\Gamma}^{n}_{01}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2M_{n}}\frac{1}{16\Lambda^{8}}\int d\Pi_{3}\,d\Pi^{\prime}_{3}\,\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{m,(i)}\,2\,{\rm Im}\left[\frac{1}{2}{({{\cal M}_{01(i)}^{\chi U\,\!}})}^{\sigma\mu\nu\tau}_{nm}\right]\,\frac{C_{01}^{(i)}(M^{12}_{1(i)}L^{34}_{(i)})_{\mu\sigma\nu}(M^{12}_{0})^{\dagger}_{\tau}}{[{p^{\prime}_{n}}^{\!\!(i)\,2}-M_{m}^{2}]}\ ,

But before we do this, we present a slightly different but equivalent method that we use to compute the

The computation of the
{D}
-loop in Fig.
5
is detailed in Appendix

, with
the momenta
{p^{\prime}_{n}}^{\!\!(A)}=p_{n}-(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3}+q_{4})

{p^{\prime}_{n}}^{\!\!(B)}=p_{n}
,
{p^{\prime}_{n}}^{\!\!(Cij)}=p_{n}-(q_{i}+q_{j})
,
and for diagram D we get the same contribution as diagram C, i.e.
L_{D}^{D}=L_{D}^{C}
.
In each process, either the
q_{1}-q_{2}
legs or the
q_{3}-q_{4}
legs can be twisted independently (since the 2
{D}
are identical)
to give us four different sub-diagram; diagrams
A
and
B
have these equal, giving an overall factor of 4 as seen above,
while for diagram C (and D) each of these are split up into the
L_{D}^{Cij}
as given above.
Although each of these subdiagrams has a negative sign due to anti-commuting two fermionic operators,
in each case the anti-symmetry of the two color indices involved undoes this minus sign.
The
{D}
-loop factor for the conjugate process are the same, i.e.
L^{c\,(i)}_{D}=L_{D}^{(i)}
of Eq. (
16
)
as argued in Appendix
E.3.1
.
Furthermore, since there are no
\gamma^{5}
in any of the
{\rm Tr}[...]
in
L^{(i)}_{D}
, they are all real,
and the phase in
\hat{\cal A}_{01}
is entirely due to the phase in
{{\cal M}_{01}^{\chi U\,\!}}
.

We recall that the expression for the
{({{\cal M}_{01(i)}^{\chi U\,\!}})}

is given in Eq. (
13
) (see also Appendix
E.3.1
).
In the tree-loop interference term for the conjugate process, the analogous factor to this,
namely
{{\cal M}_{01}^{c\,\chi U\,\!}}

{\phantom{\,}\bar{G}_{V}\phantom{\,\!}}\leftrightarrow{G_{\Lambda}}
in
{{\cal M}_{01}^{\chi U\,\!}}

2

This implies

which proves the claim we made in writing Eq. (
3

As earlier, for the numerical computation of the integrals, here too we scale all dimensionful quantities by
M_{n}
,
and take the dimensionless
({\hat{\cal M}_{01(i)}^{\chi U\,\!}})_{nm}
and
(\hat{L}^{(i)}_{D})

but with hatted momenta and masses, namely,
\hat{p}_{i}\equiv p_{i}/M_{n}
and
\hat{M}_{i}\equiv M_{i}/M_{n}

We factor out the couplings, and expand out the

{\rm Tr}[(...)({\hat{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.03125pt{\hfil/\hfil}\hss}p^{\prime}_{n}}+\hat{M}_{m})(...)(\hat{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.03125pt{\hfil/\hfil}\hss}p_{n}+\hat{M}_{n})(...)]

({\hat{\cal M}_{01(i)}^{\chi U\,\!}})_{nm}^{\sigma\mu\nu\tau}=\sum_{k,\lambda}G^{nm}_{k\lambda}\hat{{\cal M}}_{k\lambda(i)nm}^{\sigma\mu\nu\tau}\ ,

(99)

in terms of the coupling-independent traces
\hat{{\cal M}}_{k\lambda}

\displaystyle\hat{{\cal M}}_{0}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}[\gamma^{\sigma}\hat{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.03125pt{\hfil/\hfil}\hss}p^{\prime}_{n}\gamma^{\mu}(\hat{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/}^{\prime}_{U}+\hat{M}_{U})\gamma^{\nu}\hat{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.03125pt{\hfil/\hfil}\hss}p_{n}\gamma^{\tau}(\hat{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/}_{U}+\hat{M}_{U})]\ ,

\displaystyle\hat{{\cal M}}_{1\lambda}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{n}\hat{M}_{m}{\rm Tr}[\gamma^{\sigma}\gamma^{\mu}\hat{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/}^{\prime}_{U}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\tau}\hat{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/}_{U}P_{\lambda}]\ ,\quad\hat{{\cal M}}_{2\lambda}\equiv\hat{M}_{n}\hat{M}_{m}\hat{M}_{U}^{2}\,{\rm Tr}[\gamma^{\sigma}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\tau}P_{\lambda}]\ ,

\displaystyle\hat{{\cal M}}_{4\lambda}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{n}\hat{M}_{U}{\rm Tr}[\gamma^{\sigma}\hat{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.03125pt{\hfil/\hfil}\hss}p^{\prime}_{n}\gamma^{\mu}(\hat{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/}^{\prime}_{U}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\tau}P_{\lambda}+\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\tau}P_{\lambda}\hat{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/}_{U})]\ ,

(100)

where for
k=0
we are to omit
\lambda
,
and,
the coupling combinations
10
10

10

We can also define the coupling combinations

G^{nm}_{1\pm}\equiv G^{nm}_{1R}\pm G^{nm}_{1L}
and

G^{nm}_{2\pm}\equiv G^{nm}_{2L}\pm G^{nm}_{2R}
,
since
f_{01}^{nm}
picks up the combinations

2[\Im{(G_{1+})}(\hat{{\cal M}}_{1+})+\Im{(G_{2+})}(\hat{{\cal M}}_{2+})+\Re{(G_{1-})}(\hat{{\cal M}}_{1-})+\Re{(G_{2-})}(\hat{{\cal M}}_{2-})]
,
where we have defined:

\hat{{\cal M}}_{1+}\equiv\Re{(\hat{{\cal M}}_{1R})}=\Re{(\hat{{\cal M}}_{1L})}
,

\hat{{\cal M}}_{2+}\equiv\Re{(\hat{{\cal M}}_{2L})}=\Re{(\hat{{\cal M}}_{2R})}
,

\hat{{\cal M}}_{1-}\equiv\Im{(\hat{{\cal M}}_{1R})}=-\Im{(\hat{{\cal M}}_{1L})}
,

\hat{{\cal M}}_{2-}\equiv\Im{(\hat{{\cal M}}_{2L})}=-\Im{(\hat{{\cal M}}_{2R})}
.
The
\hat{\cal I}_{\pm}
definitions are in analogy with these
\hat{{\cal M}}_{\pm}
definitions.

\displaystyle G^{nm}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{Rn}\hat{g}_{Ln}\hat{g}^{*}_{Rm}\hat{g}^{*}_{Lm}\ ,

\displaystyle G^{nm}_{1R}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{g}^{2}_{Ln}\hat{g}^{*2}_{Lm}\ ,G^{nm}_{1L}=\hat{g}^{2}_{Rn}\hat{g}^{*2}_{Rm}\ ,\quad G^{nm}_{2R}=\hat{g}^{2}_{Rn}\hat{g}^{*2}_{Lm}\ ,G^{nm}_{2L}=\hat{g}^{2}_{Ln}\hat{g}^{*2}_{Rm}\ ,

\displaystyle G^{nm}_{3R}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{Ln}\hat{g}_{Rn}\hat{g}^{*2}_{Lm}\ ,G^{nm}_{3L}=\hat{g}_{Ln}\hat{g}_{Rn}\hat{g}^{*2}_{Rm}\ ,

\displaystyle G^{nm}_{4R}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{Rn}^{2}\hat{g}^{*}_{Lm}\hat{g}^{*}_{Rm}\ ,G^{nm}_{4L}=\hat{g}_{Ln}^{2}\hat{g}^{*}_{Lm}\hat{g}^{*}_{Rm}\ .

(101)

We define dimensionless coupling-independent integrals of the above traces folded in with the
{D}
-loop functions as

\displaystyle\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{k\lambda\,nm}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d\hat{\Pi}_{3}\,d\hat{\Pi}^{\prime}_{3}\,\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{(i)}\frac{1}{2}\hat{{\cal M}}_{k\lambda(i)nm}^{\sigma\mu\nu\tau}\,\frac{C^{(i)}_{01}{(\hat{L}^{(i)}_{D})}_{\mu\tau\nu\sigma}}{[({{\hat{p}}_{n}}^{\prime(i)})^{2}-\hat{M}_{m}^{2}]}\ ,

(102)

where
we define
{(L^{(i)}_{D})}\equiv|\tilde{g}|^{4}{(\hat{L}^{(i)}_{D})}
, now written with hatted momenta and masses.
We include the diagram B contribution, i.e. for
(i)\!\!=\!\!(B)
,
only for
m\!\!\neq\!\!n
(i.e.
nm\!\!=\!\!12
for
{\cal X}_{1}
decay, and
nm\!\!=\!\!21
for
{\cal X}_{2}
decay)
for the reasons already explained on p.
3.2
.

In terms of these integrals and couplings we define

\displaystyle f_{01}^{nm}=|\tilde{g}|^{4}\sum_{k,\lambda}\,{\rm Im}\left[G^{nm}_{k\lambda}\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{k\lambda\,nm}\right]\ ,

(103)

where for
k=0
we are to omit
\lambda
.
We can write Eq. (
14
) now as in Eq. (
17
).

An analysis of the superficial degree of divergence suggests that only
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{0\,nm}
could be potentially UV divergent,
assuming that the
1/\Lambda^{2}
in the operators is resolved in a gauge invariant way in the UV completion.
In the extreme high-energy limit, the masses can be ignored, and in this limit, when summed over
m
,
the structure of the
G^{nm}_{0}
is such that the unitarity of the
{\cal U}
ensures that this goes to zero,
analogous to the GIM-cancellation in the SM.
This implies that the
f_{01}
is finite with no UV divergences present.
Furthermore, using the unitarity of
{\cal U}
we can show that
G_{0}^{nm}
is always real
\forall(n,m)
.
Also,
\hat{\cal M}_{0}
is always real and therefore
k=0
does not contribute to
f_{01}^{nm}
.
All other
k
have at least one
M_{n}
or
M_{m}
factor, which indicates that at least one Majorana mass insertion is required
to generate a nonzero
{\cal A}_{B}
, as we expect.

We compute the traces in Eqs. (
16
) and (
100
) leading to dot-products of the 4-momenta involved.
Since these dot-products and the 3-body phase-space and loop integration measures are Lorentz invariant,
we can evaluate them in any convenient frame.
We discuss in Appendix
F
full details on the frames we use, the choice of independent momenta in them,
the limits of integration,
and the phase-space and loop integration measures.
As explained in full detail there,
we sample the 3-body phase-space independent momenta of the
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{U})
-sector in the
*
-frame
in which
{\bf p}_{n}^{*}={\bf q}_{U}^{*}
, implying
{\bf q}_{2}^{*}=-{\bf q}_{1}^{*}
.
We choose this frame, since in this frame, the 3-momentum radial-component upper limit of integration does not depend on the angular variables,
simplifying the sampling.
For a similar reason, we sample the independent loop momenta of the
(q_{3},q_{4},q_{U}^{\prime})
-sector in the
{\oasterisk}
-frame
in which
{\bf p}_{n}^{{\oasterisk}}={{\bf q}^{\prime}_{U}}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}
, implying
{\bf q}_{4}^{{\oasterisk}}=-{\bf q}_{3}^{{\oasterisk}}
.
For an integration sample point of the independent momenta picked in the respective frames,
we compute all the other 4-momenta using 4-momentum conservation and on-shell conditions,
and the Lorentz-invariant phase-space and loop integration-measures in those frames.
Our choice of the five independent 3-body phase space variables and the measure are as explained in Appendix
F.1
,
and of the five independent loop variables and the loop measure are as in Appendix
F.2
.

We transform all of the 4-momenta to the
{\cal X}_{n}
rest-frame (lab-frame), and evaluate all 4-momentum dot-products in this frame.
Using these dot-products, we compute the integrand of Eq. (
102
),
fold-in the above integration measures, and numerically integrate to obtain the
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{k\lambda\,nm}
.
Before discussing the numerical results, we make the following observations.

We find that the
{\rm Im}(\hat{\cal M}_{k\lambda})
are proportional to
\epsilon^{\sigma\mu\nu\tau}
contracted with the various 4-momenta
involved, and since they are all Lorentz invariant, we can evaluate them in a convenient frame, namely, either the
*
-frame or the
{\oasterisk}
-frame.
These dot-products have one
0^{\rm th}
component
(E)
and a scalar triple product of the other three 3-momenta
that is odd in the loop momenta azimuthal angles
\phi_{3},\phi^{\prime}_{U}
, and when we integrate over these angles, we get zero.
Thus all the
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{k\lambda\,nm}
are real, and therefore from Eq. (
103
) we note that
only the
{\rm Im}(G^{nm}_{k\lambda})
are picked up in generating the
{\cal A}_{B}
.

We consider here some limits in which we expect
{\cal A}_{B}
to be zero and see if our formalism is consistent with that.
We know from the Sakharov conditions that the generation of a nonzero
{\cal A}_{B}
requires
C
and
CP
violation.
We found the following conditions for these to be good symmetries.
First, we found that
C
-invariance is present if and only if
\hat{g}_{L_{n}}^{*}=\hat{g}_{R_{n}}
.
If this condition holds, the following are true for
f_{01}^{nm}
in Eq. (
103
):
(a)
G_{0}^{nm}
is real, and since
\hat{\cal M}_{0}
is real,
k=0
does not contribute in
f_{01}^{nm}
, and,
(b) for
k=\{1,2,3,4\}
,
G_{kR}=G_{kL}^{*}
and since
\hat{\cal M}_{kR}=\hat{\cal M}_{kL}^{*}
,
we have
(G_{kL}\hat{\cal M}_{kL}+G_{kR}\hat{\cal M}_{kR})
is real, and therefore these also do not contribute to
f_{01}^{nm}
.
Thus,
f_{01}^{nm}=0
in the
C
-invariance limit, i.e.
{\cal A}_{B}=0
in this limit.
Next, we found that
CP
-invariance is present if and only if
\hat{g}_{L_{n}},\hat{g}_{R_{n}}
are real, in which case all the
G^{nm}_{k\lambda}
are real.
In this limit,
k=0
does not contribute to
f_{01}
since
\hat{\cal M}_{0}
is real.
For all other
k
,
\hat{\cal M}_{kR}+\hat{\cal M}_{kL}
is real and therefore these do not contribute to
f_{01}
either,
and we have
{\cal A}_{B}=0
in the
CP
-invariance limit.
Next, we consider the “Dirac limit”, i.e. the
\tilde{M}_{L,R}=0
,
whence
{\cal U}_{an}=((i,1),(-i,1))/\sqrt{2}
,
where the
i
in the first column ensure that the mass eigenvalues are positive.
In
G_{k\lambda}
, this
i
always appears twice, giving
\pm 1
.
So the phase dependence is potentially only due to
\phi_{L}
, the only physical phase present in the Dirac limit.

k=0
does not contribute in
f_{01}
since
G_{0}
and
\hat{\cal M}_{0}
are real.
For the other
k
, the
G_{1L},G_{2L}
are insensitive to this phase,
the
G_{2L}
and
G_{2R}
are conjugate with respect to
\phi_{L}
and this phase does not contribute when summed over
L,R
,
the
G_{3},G_{4}
give zero with this
{\cal U}
when summed over the
m
(which can be done since
M_{m}
are degenerate in the Dirac limit).
Thus, we see that
{\cal A}_{B}=0
in the Dirac limit.
As mentioned already, it is reassuring that our formalism passes these nontrivial checks.

Following the method already discussed above, we use the
\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}
and
\hat{L}_{12{}}
written in terms of dot-products of the 4-momenta involved.
For each 4-momentum sample-point of the numerical integration,
we compute these dot-products and the phase-space measure,
and numerically integrate to obtain the LO integrals
{\cal I}_{0}^{n}
of Eq. (
95
).
We then fold-in the couplings to obtain the
f_{00}^{n}
from Eq. (
96
).
We then compute the LO decay width
\Gamma_{0}^{n}
from Eq. (
10
),
omitting the
(M_{\chi}^{5}/\Lambda^{4})
factor in our numerical calculation, which is to be included later.
We obtain the tree-loop interference term by computing the dot-products for the traces in
({\hat{\cal M}_{01(i)}^{\chi U\,\!}})_{nm}
in Eq. (
100
)
and in the
{D}
-loop function
{(\hat{L}^{(i)}_{D})}
in Eq. (
16
),
fold in the phase-space and loop measures,
and numerically integrate to obtain
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{k\lambda\,nm}
of Eq. (
102
).
We then include the couplings and compute the loop function
f_{01}^{nm}
from Eq. (
103
).
We then obtain the tree-loop interference decay width difference
\Delta\hat{\Gamma}_{01}^{n}
from Eq. (
17
),
and the baryon asymmetry
{\cal A}_{B}
from Eq. (
18
),
omitting in our numerical calculation the
(M_{\chi}^{9}/\Lambda^{8})
factor in the former and the
(M_{\chi}/\Lambda)^{4}
factor in the latter, which are to be included later.

For the BP-A, single operator contributions, we find the following values for the integrals.
The LO integrals
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}
are (
\times 10^{-5}
):

{{{\cal I}_{0}^{1}}}_{LL}=0.0372
,
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{1}}}_{LR}=-0.035
,
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{2}}}_{LL}=0.88
,
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{2}}}_{LR}=-0.61
.
The tree-loop interference integrals
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{k\lambda\,nm}
are (
\times 10^{-9}
):

\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{0\,21}=-2.41
,
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{1R\,21}=-0.64
,
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{2R\,21}=-0.3
,
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{3R\,21}=0.88
,
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{4R\,21}=1.1
,
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{4R\,22}=0.03
, and we have
\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{kL\,nm}=\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{kR\,nm}
.
These integrals are determined with a
sampling error better than about a few percent
and integrals with values less than
10^{-11}
are not shown.
As already discussed (see p.
G.2
), we find that the imaginary parts of these integrals are zero.

We can trace the reason for the suppression in
\hat{f}_{00}

to a 3-body phase-space suppression leading to a suppressed weight in the measure
due to
{q}_{U_{r}},{q_{1}^{*}}_{\!r}\sim{\cal O}(0.1)
for the decay kinematics in BP-A.
Similarly,
we can trace the reason for the suppressed
\hat{f}_{01}

to a product of the suppression factor in the 3-body phase-space as above in the LO integrals,
and another
10^{-3}
factor from the suppressed weight in the loop-integral measure
due to the cut of the loop-diagram picking out similar sizes of loop-momenta as phase-space momenta, i.e. suppressed as
{q^{\prime}_{U}}_{\!r},{q_{3}}_{r}^{\!\!{\oasterisk}}\sim{\cal O}(0.1)
.

For our parameter choices here, we find that the diagram-B contribution is about 75 times
the contribution from diagrams A, C, D combined. One enhancement in diagram B is a relative color factor of 3,
while another relative factor of about 10 can be traced to the different Lorentz structures in Eq. (
16
).

G.3
The multiple operator decay rate difference

We compute the multiple operator matrix element similar to the single operator contribution above.
We give the one-loop measure
d\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
in Appendix
F.2.2
,
and the 3-body phase-space measure
d\Pi_{3}
, as already discussed, in Appendix
F.1
.
The dimensionless hatted versions of these are obtained by scaling all the momenta and masses by
M_{n}
.
Requiring both the left cut and the right-cut in this case implies that the upper limit
{q}_{U_{r}}^{(max)}

is now modified as discussed in Appendix
F.2.2
.

As mentioned, we find no asymmetry from diagrams
A_{1}^{\prime(2),(3)}
in Fig.
8
,
as we could anticipate from there being no arrow clash in these diagrams.
This is shown in Appendix
E.4.2
.

We give next the numerical values we obtain for the multiple-operator contribution in BP-B.
The LO integrals
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}
are (
\times 10^{-5}
):

{{{\cal I}_{0}^{1}}}_{LL}=7.9
,
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{1}}}_{LR}=-0.85
,
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{2}}}_{LL}=8.06
,
{{{\cal I}_{0}^{2}}}_{LR}=-0.72
.

As explained already, the smallness in the value of the rate asymmetry in this case is the result of suppressions due to
the 3-body phase-space weight of Eq. (
86
) (without the
1/(2\pi)^{5}
) of
\sim 10^{-3}
,
the loop measure weight of Eq. (
89
) (without the
1/(2\pi)^{2}
) of
\sim 10^{-2}
,
and,
the dimensionless
{\cal A}_{01}^{\prime(1)}
(without the
1/\Lambda^{6}
) of
\sim 10^{-4}
including the (imaginary part of) coupling factors.

G.4
The single operator scattering rate difference

The
d\Pi_{2}
is the final-state 2-body phase-space element,
and we give full details of our choice of independent variables in Sec.
F.3
.
As explained there, we appeal to the frame invariance of the phase space measure and take the
d\Pi_{2}
independent variables,
namely
c_{1}\equiv\cos{\theta}_{1}
and
\phi_{1}
, in the center-of-momentum (CM) frame.

We strip out the couplings and write in terms of the traces as in the decay case,
with the dimensionless hatted momenta and masses, i.e.
\hat{p_{i}}\equiv p_{i}/M_{n}
and
\hat{M}_{i}\equiv M_{i}/M_{n}
,
and compute integrals analogous to those in Eq. (
95
),
namely,

{\cal I}^{n(\sigma)}_{0\,{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}}=\frac{1}{2\hat{E}_{n}2\hat{E}_{i}}\int d\hat{\Pi}_{2}\left[-\frac{1}{4}(\hat{{\cal M}}^{00}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}})_{nn}^{\mu\tau}\,C_{00}\,(\hat{L}_{12{}})_{\mu\tau}\right]_{(q_{i}\ {\rm cross})}\ .

(104)

Unlike in the decay case, here we include the
1/(2\hat{E}_{n}2\hat{E}_{i})
factors in the definition of the integrals since
this factor should be in the integrand of the thermal averaging (cf. Sec.
4.1.1
).

We fold back the couplings analogous to Eq. (
96
)
to obtain

f_{00}^{n(\sigma)}=|\tilde{g}|^{2}\left[(|\hat{g}_{L_{n}}|^{2}+|\hat{g}_{R_{n}}|^{2})\,{\cal I}^{n(\sigma)}_{0\,{LL}}+(\hat{g}_{L_{n}}^{*}\hat{g}_{R_{n}}+\hat{g}_{R_{n}}^{*}\hat{g}_{L_{n}})\,{\cal I}^{n(\sigma)}_{0\,{LR}}\right]\ ,

(105)

and compute from this the LO cross sections in Eq. (
25
).

We move next to the tree-loop interference contribution in scattering.
In analogy with Eqs. (
98
)
we write

\Delta\hat{\sigma}_{01}^{n}\,v=\frac{1}{2E_{n}2E_{i}}\,\frac{1}{16\Lambda^{8}}\sum_{m}\int d\Pi_{2}\,d\tilde{\Pi}^{\prime}_{3}\,\frac{1}{2}\sum_{(i)}\,\left\{2\,{\rm Im}\left[-\frac{1}{4}{({{\cal M}_{01(i)}^{\chi U\,\!}})}^{\sigma\mu\nu\tau}_{nm}\right]\,\frac{C_{01}^{(i)}(M^{12}_{1(i)}L^{34}_{(i)})_{\mu\sigma\nu}(M^{12}_{0})^{\dagger}_{\tau}}{[{p^{\prime}_{n}}^{\!\!(i)\,2}-M_{m}^{2}]}\right\}_{(q_{i}\ {\rm cross})}\ .

(106)

Here,
[d\tilde{\Pi}^{\prime}_{3}]
is the measure for integration over the loop momenta, and we give its details in Sec.
F.4
.
As earlier in Eq. (
102
), we strip off the couplings and define dimensionless integrals

\displaystyle\hat{\cal I}^{(01)(\sigma)}_{k\lambda\,nm}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\hat{E}_{n}2\hat{E}_{i}}\,\int d\hat{\Pi}_{2}\,d\hat{\tilde{\Pi}}^{\prime}_{3}\,\frac{1}{2}\sum_{(i)}\left\{-\frac{1}{4}\hat{{\cal M}}_{k\lambda(i)nm}^{\sigma\mu\nu\tau}\,\frac{C^{(i)}_{01}{(\hat{L}^{(i)}_{D})}_{\mu\tau\nu\sigma}}{[({{\hat{p}}_{n}}^{\prime(i)})^{2}-\hat{M}_{m}^{2}]}\right\}_{(q_{i}\ {\rm cross})}\ .

(107)

We fold back in the couplings analogous to Eq. (
103
) to obtain

f_{01}^{nm(\sigma)}=|\tilde{g}|^{4}\sum_{k,\lambda}\,{\rm Im}\left[G^{nm}_{k\lambda}\hat{\cal I}^{(01)(\sigma)}_{k\lambda\,nm}\right]\ ,

(108)

for each of the SC-1 and SC-2.

For obtaining the thermally averaged LO cross section,
we first thermally average taking the
{\cal I}^{n(\sigma)}_{0\,{}}
of Eq. (
104
) as the
\Theta
of Eq. (
48
)
to obtain the thermally averaged integrals
\langle{\cal I}^{n(\sigma)}_{0\,{}}\rangle
.
In order to obtain the thermally averaged cross section difference and the thermally averaged baryon asymmetry,
we thermally average in the sense of Eq. (
48
) using Eq. (
107
)
and setting
\Theta=\hat{\cal I}^{(01)(\sigma)}_{k\lambda\,nm}

to obtain the corresponding thermally averaged integrals
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{(01)(\sigma)}_{k\lambda\,nm}\rangle
.

We evaluate numerically the thermal averages of the integrals of Eqs. (
104
) and (
107
),
generating the independent 4-momenta samples in the various frames as discussed earlier.
For illustration, we give here the values of the thermal average integrals for the benchmark point BP-A
laid out on p.
3.2.1
.
The tree-level thermal average integrals
\langle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}^{(\sigma)}\rangle
for
x\!\!=\!\!1/2

with the UV propagator correction factor present for
M_{\chi}/\Lambda\!\!=\!\!1/10
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}=1/10
,
for (SC-1, SC-2) respectively are:

\langle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{1}}}_{LL}^{(\sigma)}\rangle=(2.45,84.1)
,

\langle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{1}}}_{LR}^{(\sigma)}\rangle=(-0.008,1.1)
,

\langle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{2}}}_{LL}^{(\sigma)}\rangle=(2.45,83.9)
,

\langle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{2}}}_{LR}^{(\sigma)}\rangle=(-0.007,0.88)
.
The tree-loop interference thermal average integrals
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{k\lambda\,nm}\rangle

for
x\!\!=\!\!1/2
for (SC-1, SC-2) respectively are (
\times 10^{-3}
):

\langle\hat{\cal I}^{(01)(\sigma)}_{0\,21}\rangle=(-3,-36)
,
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{(01)(\sigma)}_{1R\,21}\rangle=(-1.2,-23)
,
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{(01)(\sigma)}_{3R\,21}\rangle=(0.8,17)
,
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{(01)(\sigma)}_{4R\,21}\rangle=(1.1,22)
, We have shown only the
\lambda\!\!=\!\!R
integral values,
since we find
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{kL\,nm}\rangle=\langle\hat{\cal I}^{(01)}_{kR\,nm}\rangle
.
These integrals are determined with a
sampling error better than about 20 %,
and integrals with values less than
10^{-4}
are not shown.

For these parameter choices, with the UV propagator correction present,
the diagram B contribution of Fig.
5
(but crossed for scattering) is dominant,
being about 1000 times the contributions from diagrams A,C,D.
We trace this to a suppression from the
{\cal X}_{m}
propagator of about a factor of 50 in diagram A,C,D,
while it enhances the diagram B contribution by a factor of about 5,
thus largely explaining the relatively suppressed A,C,D contributions.

G.5
The multiple operator scattering rate difference

We give details on the phase space variables and measure in Appendix
F.3
,
and,
the loop integration variables and measure in Appendix
F.4
.
We generate the independent loop and phase-space momenta of the integration sampling points in the CM frame and compute all the
4-momentum dot-products in the CM frame.

We evaluate the integrals numerically,
and for illustration we give for BP-B, some sample numerical values for the thermally averaged integrals.
With the UV state propagator correction included with
M_{\chi}/\Lambda\!\!=\!\!1/10
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}\!\!=\!\!1/2
,
the tree-level thermal average integrals
\langle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{n}}}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}^{(\sigma)}\rangle
, for
x\!\!=\!\!1/2
,
for (SC-1, SC-2) respectively are:

\langle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{1}}}_{LL}^{(\sigma)}\rangle\!=\!(2.45,15.7)
,

\langle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{1}}}_{LR}^{(\sigma)}\rangle\!=\!(-1.1\times 10^{-3},0.027)
,

\langle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{2}}}_{LL}^{(\sigma)}\rangle\!=\!(2.45,15.7)
,

\langle{{{\cal I}_{0}^{2}}}_{LR}^{(\sigma)}\rangle\!=\!(-9\times 10^{-4},0.023)
.

Denoting the multiple operator scattering cross section difference as
\langle\Delta\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}_{01}\,v\rangle
,
we can compare, for example for the
(M_{\chi}/\Lambda,\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi})\!\!=\!\!(1/10,1/2)
case here
with the single operator contribution
\langle\Delta\hat{\sigma}_{01}\,v\rangle
in the previous section.
Including the
M_{\chi}/\Lambda
scaling factors but suppressing the coupling dependence, we find,

\langle\Delta\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}_{01}\,v\rangle/\langle\Delta\hat{\sigma}_{01}\,v\rangle\!\sim\!(2\pi^{2})(\Lambda/M_{\chi})^{2}\langle\hat{f}_{01}^{{}^{\prime}(\sigma)}\rangle/\langle\hat{f}_{01}^{(\sigma)}\rangle\sim 10^{3}
.
Similarly, comparing the multiple operator to single operator scattering baryon asymmetry, for
M_{\chi}/\Lambda\!\!=\!\!1/10
,
we find

\hat{\cal A}_{B}^{\prime(\sigma)}/\hat{\cal A}_{B}^{(\sigma)}\!\sim\!2\pi^{2}(\Lambda/M_{\chi})^{2}\langle\hat{f}_{01}^{{}^{\prime}(\sigma)}\rangle/\langle\hat{f}_{01}^{(\sigma)}\rangle\cdot\langle\hat{f}_{00}^{(\sigma)}\rangle/\langle\hat{f}_{00}^{{}^{\prime}(\sigma)}\rangle\!\sim\!10^{4}
.

G.6
The
\Delta B=-2

{U}{D}{D}\rightarrow{U}^{c}{D}^{c}{D}^{c}
scattering rate

Taking the matrix element mod-squared averaged over initial state spins and summed over final spins,
and folding in phase-space we compute the
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}
in Eq. (
34
),
by decomposing this computation into various integrals similar to those in Eq. (
102
).
These integrals are

\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{k\lambda\,nm}\equiv\frac{1}{(2E^{\prime}_{U})(2E_{3})(2E_{4})}\int d\hat{\Pi}_{3}\,\sum_{(i)}\frac{1}{8}\hat{{\cal M}}_{k\lambda(i)nm}^{\sigma\mu\nu\tau}\,\frac{C^{(i)}_{\bar{B}B}{(\hat{L}^{(i)}_{\bar{B}B})}_{\mu\nu\tau\sigma}}{[({{\hat{p}}_{n}}^{\prime})^{2}-\hat{M}_{n}^{2}+i\hat{M}_{n}\hat{\Gamma}_{n}]\,[({{\hat{p}}_{m}}^{\prime})^{2}-\hat{M}_{m}^{2}+i\hat{M}_{m}\hat{\Gamma}_{m}]}

(109)

where
for the S-channel-like contribution we have
{p_{n}}^{\!\!\!\prime~(S)}\!=\!p_{n}
,
while for the T-channel-like contribution we have
{p_{n}}^{\!\!\!\prime~(T)}\!=\!q^{\prime}_{U}-(q_{1}+q_{2})
,
the index is over
(i)\!\!=\!\!SS,ST,TS,TT
contributions from the mod-square of the sum of the S- and T-channel-like contributions,
and the
\hat{{\cal M}}_{k\lambda(i)nm}
are as in Eq. (
100
) with the associated couplings as in Eq. (
101
).
The
DD
-loop part is given as

(\hat{L}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bar{B}B})_{\mu\nu\tau\sigma}=4C^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bar{B}B}\ {\rm Tr}[\gamma^{\mu}(\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/_{1}+M_{D})\gamma^{\nu}(\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/_{2}-M_{D})]\ {\rm Tr}[\gamma^{\tau}(\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/_{4}+M_{D})\gamma^{\sigma}(\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.00002pt{\hfil$q$\hfil}\hss}/_{3}-M_{D})]\ ,

(110)

where the indices are as shown for
SS
,
exchanged as
\mu\leftrightarrow\sigma
and
\nu\leftrightarrow\tau
for
TT
,

\mu\leftrightarrow\sigma
for
ST
,

\nu\leftrightarrow\tau
for
TS
,
and,
the color factors are
C^{\scriptscriptstyle(SS),(TT)}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bar{B}B}\!=\!6
as in diagram-B, and
C^{\scriptscriptstyle(ST),(TS)}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bar{B}B}\!=\!2
as in diagram-A (cf. Eq. (
16
)).
We then compute the analog of Eq. (
103
), but now picking up the real part, as

\displaystyle f_{\scriptscriptstyle\bar{B}B}^{nm}=|\tilde{g}|^{4}\sum_{k,\lambda}\,{\rm Re}\left[G^{nm}_{k\lambda}\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{k\lambda\,nm}\right]\ .

(111)

G.6.1
Thermally averaged
UDD\rightarrow U^{c}D^{c}D^{c}
scattering rate

Here we review aspects of the initial state, the center-of-momentum (CM) momentum variables, and thermal averaging
in the
UDD\rightarrow U^{c}D^{c}D^{c}
process of Sec.
5
,
and simplify the expression for its thermally averaged scattering rate
\langle\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle
in Eq. (
36
).
We exploit the parallels in kinematics with the initial state integration in thermal averaging
with the loop integration in the
{\cal X}_{n}
decay tree-loop interference calculation in Sec.
3
,
and pick independent momentum variables as there.

The measure for integration over the initial state momenta
q^{\prime}_{U},q_{3},q_{4}
,
in the notation
[dq]\equiv d^{3}q/(2\pi)^{3}\,1/(2E_{q})
, is

\langle\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle=\frac{1}{n_{\chi}^{(0)}}\int[dq^{\prime}_{U}][dq_{3}][dq_{4}]\frac{d^{4}p_{\scriptscriptstyle CM}}{(2\pi)^{4}}(\Pi_{i}f_{Q_{i}}^{(0)})\ |{\cal P}_{\chi}|^{2}\ (2\pi)^{4}\delta^{(4)}(p_{\scriptscriptstyle CM}-(q^{\prime}_{U}+q_{3}+q_{4}))\,|\widetilde{\cal M}^{\prime}_{0}|^{2}\ d\Pi_{3}\ ,

(112)

where

{\cal P}_{\chi}\!=\!1/({p^{\prime}_{n}}^{2}-M_{n}^{2}+iM_{n}\Gamma_{n})
is the
{\cal X}_{n}
propagator with
p^{\prime}_{n}
being the
{\cal X}_{n}
momentum,
we define
{\cal M}^{\prime}_{0}=\widetilde{\cal M}^{\prime}_{0}\,{\cal P}_{\chi}
,
the thermal (equilibrium) distribution for each of the initial
Q_{i}\!=\!\{U,D,D\}
is
f_{Q_{i}}^{(0)}=e^{-E_{i}/T}
,

d\Pi_{3}
is the final state phase space measure as detailed earlier (cf. Sec.
F.2.1
),
and
we show an integration over the CM momentum
p_{\scriptscriptstyle CM}\!=\!q^{\prime}_{U}+q_{3}+q_{4}
along with the energy-momentum conserving
\delta
-function,
the zeroth component of which enforces
p_{\scriptscriptstyle CM}^{0}\!=\!E^{\prime}_{U}+E_{3}+E_{4}
, which implies
\Pi_{i}f_{Q_{i}}^{(0)}=e^{-p_{\scriptscriptstyle CM}^{0}/T}
.
In this work, we specialize to the
SS
contribution discussed in Sec.
5
, with
p_{CM}\!=\!p^{\prime}_{n}\!=\!p_{n}
.

We obtain the
{\cal X}_{n}
pole contribution
by replacing
|{\cal P}_{\chi}|^{2}\rightarrow\pi/(M_{n}\Gamma_{n})\ \delta({p_{n}^{0}}^{2}-E_{n}^{2})
,
with
E_{n}\!=\!\sqrt{{p_{n}}_{r}^{2}+M_{n}^{2}}
.
This amounts to making the narrow width approximation for the
{\cal X}_{n}
.
We use the representation

\delta(x^{2}-a^{2})=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}~[(\epsilon/\pi)/((x^{2}-a^{2})^{2}+\epsilon^{2})]
with
\epsilon=M_{n}\Gamma_{n}
.
We notice that the integral is singular and is regulated by
\Gamma_{n}
.
The
\delta
-function sets
p_{n}^{0}\!=\!E_{n}
putting
{\cal X}_{n}
onshell, giving
\Pi_{i}f_{Q_{i}}^{(0)}=e^{-E_{n}/T}=f^{(0)}_{\chi}
.
We perform as earlier in Sec.
F.2.1
the
q_{4}
integral to enforce the energy-momentum conserving
\delta
-function,
and follow the same procedure as there, leading to the
d\Pi_{3}^{\prime}
.
Carrying out the
p_{n}^{0}
integral to enforce the
\delta
-function, we obtain

\langle\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle_{\rm pole}=\int d\Pi_{3}d\Pi^{\prime}_{3}\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{\pi}{M_{n}\Gamma_{n}}\left|\widetilde{\cal M}^{\prime}_{0}\right|^{2}\ \left[(4\pi)\frac{d{p_{n}}_{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle\!\!\!\!(L)}{{p_{n}}_{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle\!\!\!\!(L)}}^{2}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{1}{2E_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(L)}}\frac{f^{(0)}_{\chi}}{n_{\chi}^{(0)}}\right]\ ,

(113)

where we are integrating over the CM-frame momentum in the lab-frame
p_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(L)}
with a trivial angular dependence.
Owing to the Lorentz invariance of
d\Pi_{3}
,
d\Pi^{\prime}_{3}
,
|\widetilde{\cal M}^{\prime}_{0}|^{2}
, we are free to evaluate these in convenient frames
(eg.
{\cal X}_{n}
rest-frame, *-frame,
{\oasterisk}
-frame, cf. Sec.
3
)
and they would be independent of any boost of the CM-frame to
p_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(L)}
;
the quantity in
[...]
has a dependence on
p_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(L)}
(and on no other momenta)
and this factor can be integrated separately over
p_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(L)}
, giving
\langle 1/(2E_{n})\rangle_{\!P}
,
the
P
subscript reminding us of the
1/n_{\chi}^{(0)}
normalization making it like a probability distribution
(cf. Appendix
B
).
We can use here the result of Eq. (
50
) (and shown in Fig. (
28
)) to get
\langle\hat{M_{n}}/\hat{E}_{n}\rangle_{\!P}\!=\!K_{1}(\hat{M_{n}}x)/K_{2}(\hat{M_{n}}x)
.
Thus, in the narrow width approximation, we have

\langle\hat{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{0}\rangle_{\rm pole}=\frac{1}{2\hat{M}_{n}}\frac{K_{1}(\hat{M}_{n}x)}{K_{2}(\hat{M}_{n}x)}\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{0}\ ;\quad{\rm where}\ \hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{0}=\frac{1}{2\hat{M}_{n}\hat{\Gamma}_{n}}\ \int d\hat{\Pi}_{3}d\hat{\Pi}^{\prime}_{3}\left|\widetilde{\cal M}^{\prime}_{0}\right|^{2}\ ,

(114)

now written with dimensionless variables, with the
\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{0}
being
x
independent.
We follow the same procedure as for the tree-loop interference term in Sec.
3
and evaluate the integral above numerically.

We obtain the nonpole contribution by subtracting out from
the integrand of Eq. (
112
) the pole contribution, i.e. the
\delta
-function discussed above.
In other words, we take instead of
|{\cal P}_{\chi}|^{2}
in Eq. (
112
) the subtracted form

\left|{\cal P}_{\chi}\right|^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(-)}\equiv|{\cal P}_{\chi}|^{2}-\pi/(M_{n}\Gamma_{n})\ \delta({p_{n}^{0}}^{2}-E_{n}^{2})
using the representation of the delta function given above.
Using the subtracted propagator ensures that the integrand is nonsingular and leads to a more stable numerical integration
over
p_{n}^{0}
.
For the integration, we trade
p_{n}^{0}
for
\tilde{M}_{n}
, with
dp_{n}^{0}=d\tilde{M}_{n}\tilde{M}_{n}/\tilde{E}_{n}
where
\tilde{E}_{n}=\sqrt{{p_{n}}_{r}^{2}+\tilde{M}_{n}^{2}}
, the
{\cal X}_{n}
now being offshell with virtual mass
\tilde{M}_{n}
.
Also, owing to the zeroth component
\delta
-function (energy conservation),
we have here
\Pi_{i}f_{Q_{i}}^{(0)}=e^{-\tilde{E}_{n}/T}\equiv\tilde{f}^{(0)}(\tilde{E}_{n})
.
The numerical evaluation of the integrals is similar to the pole contribution case above,
with the extra integration over
\tilde{M}_{n}
now added, again taking the
{\cal X}_{n}
momentum in the lab-frame (
p_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(L)}
) as above.
Putting these together we have for the nonpole (remainder) contribution

\displaystyle\langle\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle_{\rm nonpole}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d\Pi_{3}d\Pi^{\prime}_{3}\frac{d\tilde{M}_{n}}{2\pi}2\tilde{M}_{n}\,\left|\widetilde{\cal M}^{\prime}_{0}\right|^{2}\,\left|{\cal P}_{\chi}\right|^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(-)}\,(4\pi)\frac{d{p_{n}}_{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle\!\!\!\!(L)}{{p_{n}}_{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle\!\!\!\!(L)}}^{2}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{1}{2\tilde{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle(L)}_{n}}\frac{\tilde{f}^{(0)}(\tilde{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle(L)}_{n})}{n_{\chi}^{(0)}}\ ,

(115)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt{\rm where}\ \left|{\cal P}_{\chi}\right|^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(-)}=\left|\frac{1}{\tilde{M}_{n}^{2}-M_{n}^{2}+iM_{n}\Gamma_{n}}\right|^{2}-\frac{\pi}{M_{n}\Gamma_{n}}\ \frac{\epsilon}{\pi}\frac{1}{(\tilde{M}_{n}^{2}-M_{n}^{2})^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\ .

We have checked that the numerically evaluated nonpole integrals are not very sensitive to the choices of
\Gamma_{n}
and
\epsilon
,
and for numerical stability we conveniently choose the corresponding scaled variables to be
1/10
and
1/100
respectively.

We compute the thermal average of Eq. (
109
)
by supplying the
d^{3}\hat{p}_{Q_{i}}/(2\pi)^{3}~f_{Q_{i}}^{(0)}
factors for the initial state
as required by Eq. (
36
) and integrate
to obtain
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{k\lambda\,nm}\rangle
.

The
\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{k\lambda\,nm}
values before thermal averaging, for
\tilde{M}_{n}\!=\!1
,
{p_{n}}_{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle\!\!\!\!(L)}\!=\!0
,
{q^{\prime}_{U}}_{\!r}\!=\!0
,
for BP-A are

\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{1\lambda\,11}\!=\!0.12
,
\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{1\lambda\,22}\!=\!0.14
,

\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{2\lambda\,11}\!=\!0.1
,
\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{2\lambda\,22}\!=\!0.12
,
which for
g_{L}\!=\!1
,
g_{R}\!=\!0
gives
\hat{\sigma}_{0}^{\prime}\!=\!0.004
;
while for BP-B are

\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{1\lambda\,11}\!=\!1.7
,
\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{1\lambda\,22}\!=\!2
,

\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{2\lambda\,11}\!=\!0.21
,
\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{2\lambda\,22}\!=\!0.24
.

We find the thermally averaged integral
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{k\lambda\,nm}\rangle

(pole, nonpole) values for BP-A to be

\langle\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{1\lambda\,11}\rangle\!=\!(4.2\times 10^{-5},2.36)
,
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{1\lambda\,22}\rangle\!=\!(0.15,3.5)
,

\langle\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{2\lambda\,11}\rangle\!=\!(3.8\times 10^{-5},3.1\times 10^{-2})
,
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{2\lambda\,22}\rangle\!=\!(0.086,0.046)
.
For BP-B we find

\langle\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{1\lambda\,11}\rangle\!=\!(1.16,2.5)
,
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{1\lambda\,22}\rangle\!=\!(8.84,3.7)
,

\langle\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{2\lambda\,11}\rangle\!=\!(0.023,6.2\times 10^{-4})
,
\langle\hat{\cal I}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\bar{B}B)}_{2\lambda\,22}\rangle\!=\!(0.12,9.3\times 10^{-4})
.

Appendix H
CPT and Unitarity Relations in Perturbation Theory

We discuss here how the content of Eq. (
53
) works in perturbation theory,
which is quite intricate.
In Sec.
3
we computed the decay width difference with Feynman diagrams at order
\mathcal{O}(g^{4})

for the single operator contributions represented by diagrams A,B,C,D,
which are the interference terms between the loop diagrams A
1
,B
1
,C
1
,D
1
with the tree-level diagram.
Since in these diagrams the intermediate
UDD
are anyway being cut to obtain the strong phase,
we can obtain the inverse decay asymmetry by repositioning the sub-factors from there.
Eq. (
53
) suggests to us that to obtain the inverse decay asymmetry,
we are to start with the
UDD\rightarrow U^{c}D^{c}D^{c}
scattering channel and focus on diagrams in which an intermediate
{\cal X}
can go on-shell
giving the required RIS piece.
Such an onshell
{\cal X}
propagator puts a
1/\Gamma_{\cal X}
factor, which at LO is
1/\mathcal{O}(g^{2})
,
and therefore we must also look at scattering diagrams at
\mathcal{O}(g^{6})
since we can obtain a

\mathcal{O}(g^{6})/\mathcal{O}(g^{2})\!\!=\!\!\mathcal{O}(g^{4})
decay asymmetry contribution.
An example of an
\mathcal{O}(g^{6})
contribution
is shown in Fig.
25
as the cut in the
UDD\rightarrow UDD
forward amplitude,
where we start with diagram-A, move parts of the diagram around for it to be the interference term
between loop and tree diagrams of the
UDD\rightarrow U^{c}D^{c}D^{c}
scattering channel
and insert a
{\cal X}
2-point function
\Omega^{c}+\Omega
(see Appendix
A
) We show this for the process; the conjugate process is obtained by reversing all arrows in the diagram,
i.e. each of the fields is replaced with its conjugate,
and the baryon asymmetry is of course given by the difference of these.

Figure 25:
The
DDU\rightarrow DDU
forward scattering amplitude showing the diagram-A like tree-loop interference term
whose RIS piece yields the inverse decay contribution. The dashed curves show the cuts.

The part to the right of the
U^{c}D^{c}D^{c}
cut in
\Omega^{c},\Omega
is the tree-level scattering amplitude,
while the part to the left of this cut is the loop amplitude.
The RIS contribution is obtained when the
{\cal X}_{n}
on the left goes onshell.
This RIS contribution differs from the pure scattering contribution by the additional factor shown in
[...]

in the figure.
This factor is
i(\Omega+\Omega^{c})i(\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.03125pt{\hfil/\hfil}\hss}p_{n}+M_{n})/(p^{2}-M_{n}^{2}+iM_{n}\Gamma_{\cal X})
.
For the contribution to survive we must cut
\Omega,\Omega^{c}
giving
i\hat{\Omega},i\hat{\Omega}^{c}
,
or otherwise the counterterm cancels this onshell
{\cal X}
contribution (see Appendix
A
)
For the onshell (RIS) contribution then this factor is
-i(\hat{\Omega}+\hat{\Omega}^{c})/(iM_{n}\Gamma_{\cal X}))
.
Using the fact that the total width is
\Gamma_{\cal X}=\int(\hat{\Omega}+\hat{\Omega}^{c})
,
this factor is
-\int((...)\times(\hat{\Omega}+\hat{\Omega}^{c})\times(...))/\int(\hat{\Omega}+\hat{\Omega}^{c}))
.
We take all integration measures above appropriately and do not show them explicitly.
The integral and Dirac trace in the numerator shown as
(...)
is split by the
\delta
functions of the RIS cut
into separate integral and Dirac trace factors,
one of which is exactly like the denominator, which cancels, giving -1 for the
[...]
part of the figure.
This is the origin of the minus sign in Eq. (
53
) now obtained perturbatively at
\mathcal{O}(g^{4})
,
and proves the relation at this order.
This minus sign in conjunction with the factor of 2 in the last line of Eq. (
55
)
coming from the considered scattering channel being
\Delta B\!\!=\!\!\pm 2
,
gives the RIS piece to be -2 of the first line in this equation, which overturns the sign of the first line, as required.

To understand the behavior of the scattering collision terms perturbatively, we consider the forward scattering amplitude similar to Fig.
25
,
but now modified with an initial state
Q
(
{D}
for SC-1 or
{U}
for SC-2) and a same-kind final state
Q
crossed to become intermediate states
and joined to get an additional loop.
The insertion of
(\Omega+\Omega^{c})
to obtain an
\mathcal{O}(g^{6})
contribution and the
{\cal X}_{n}
RIS gives a
\mathcal{O}(g^{2})

denominator, and leads to a
\mathcal{O}(g^{4})
asymmetry.
Following a similar logic as in the inverse decay case earlier, the above inserted factor gives a minus sign,
now understood perturbatively in the scattering case.

Appendix J
UV Completion Corrections - Numerical Details

In Appendix
D
we discuss the manner in which we handle potential UV propagator corrections in our
thermally averaged rates. Here we provide more details on the behavior of these corrections for some sample parameter choices.

In Fig.
29
we show the LO
f_{\xi\,(s,t)}
and
f_{\xi\,(s,t)}^{(00)}
for the t-channel SC-1,
and the s-channel SC-2, in the CM frame for
r_{M\Lambda}=M_{\chi}/\Lambda\!\!=\!\!1/10
.
With our parametrization of the
\xi
width,
\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}=\Gamma_{\xi}/M_{\chi}=r_{M\Lambda}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\xi}
,
we present our results for sample choices of
\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}
.
For SC-2 we show the PV for
\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}\!\!=\!\!1/2,1/10
,
which correspond to
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\xi}=1/20,1/100
respectively, for
M_{\chi}/\Lambda\!\!=\!\!1/10
.
The resonant enhancement is clearly seen, with the peak being larger for a smaller value of
\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}
.
For illustration, we have taken the mass parameters as in BP-A for
n=2
,
and set the CM frame phase space angular variables to
c_{1}^{CM}=1/2
, and
\phi_{1}^{CM}=\pi/6
.

Figure 29:

The CM frame LO UV propagator correction
f_{\xi}
for BP-A parameters,
M_{\chi}/\Lambda\!\!=\!\!1/10
,
for SC-1 (top left) and SC-2 (top right),
and
f_{\xi\,(s,t)}^{(00)}
(bottom panel) for SC-1 (solid), and
SC-2 with
\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}\!\!=\!\!1/2
(dashed) and
\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}=1/10
(dotted);
the bottom right plot zooming in on the resonance region.

We include the
f_{\xi}
factor and obtain the
{\langle\sigma\,v\rangle}
by numerical integration as detailed in Sec.
4
.
For illustration we evaluate the
{\langle\sigma\,v\rangle}
for
x=1/3
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\xi}=\Gamma_{\xi}/\Lambda=1/10
.
Compared to the effective theory value, we find the following
{\langle\sigma\,v\rangle}
numbers for scheme (a) (with scheme (b) shown in parenthesis):

for
r_{M\Lambda}=1/10
, SC-1 is 66% (70%) lower, while the SC-2 is 200% higher (33% lower);

for
r_{M\Lambda}=1/25
, SC-1 is 33% (33%) lower, while SC-2 is 600% (30%) higher;

for
r_{M\Lambda}=1/50
, SC-1 is 10% (13%) lower, while SC-2 is 65% (45%) higher;

for
r_{M\Lambda}=1/100
, SC-1 is 3% (4%) lower, while SC-2 is 10% (10%) higher.

From this we learn that
the relative difference in the thermal average cross section between the effective theory and the
UV completion A
is at most an

{\cal O}(1)
factor for
M_{\chi}/\Lambda<1/10
,
and the agreement is better than to 10% (10%) for
r_{M\Lambda}<1/100
.

For the tree-loop interference contribution of Fig.
5
,
we include the UV state
\xi
propagator correction by multiplying the
f_{\xi}(p)
for each vertex, which yields

f^{(01)}_{\xi}=f_{\xi}(p_{n}-q^{\prime}_{U})\,f_{\xi}(q^{\prime}_{U}-p_{n}^{\prime})\,f_{\xi}(p^{\prime}_{n}-q_{U})\,f_{\xi}(q_{U}-p_{n})
,
which is equivalently,

f^{(01)}_{\xi}=f_{\xi}(p_{n}-q^{\prime}_{U})\,f_{\xi}(q^{\prime}_{U}-p_{n}^{\prime})\,f^{*}_{\xi}(q_{U}-p^{\prime}_{n})\,f^{*}_{\xi}(p_{n}-q_{U})
,
and we take the PV part of each factor.
For the UV correction factor for the scattering channels we apply the crossing relations in Eq. (
23
) on this.
